Summary

Fuck. Agatha was a goner. She had a slight gap in her front teeth that made her fucking feral.

“Please welcome, last year's winner, Rio Vidal,” the voice thundered. A round of applause was heard from the room.

Rio Vidal.

She knew that name. Or, she had heard it, at least. Rio was charming, her voice soft and tender. Her fucking smile - Agatha was soon standing by the big TV screen, staring. She read the nominees, then the winner.

---

Agatha is a renowned popstar, with more fame than she ever thought possible. One drunken night, she goes on a liking-spree of Rio Vidal's posts, an up and coming puertorican, openly lesbian, singer. What could go wrong?

Notes

hello!! I got this idea and had to get it out of my system. I'll upload as soon as I'm able. Agatha is (obviously) heavily inspired by Taylor Swift, although I won't get into arguments of whether I think Taylor is queer or not. That's not up for me to discuss, I'm literally just basing Agatha off of her due to her success and private life. Rio is based off of Young Miko.

The song Agatha listens is WASSUP - it's good and I love the same bit Agatha loves. Hope you enjoy!

Chapter 1: mami, what's up?

“Agatha Evelyn Harkness!”

Fuck.

“Agatha, where the fuck are you?”

Shit.

A groan. She rolled onto her back, eyes closing more intently. She stretched on what she presumed was her bed and groaned. She could hear the footsteps of a madwoman approaching, as a bad taste summoned from her stomach all the way to the back of her throat.

“Agatha Harkness, wake the fuck up.” The door of her bedroom opened and Agatha grabbed her duvet and covered herself with it. “No, you don’t get to hide, Agatha.” Two hands yanked the material from her and she shivered from the cold air in the room. “You’re going to take a fucking shower, drink a fuckton of water, and you’re going to hand me your damn phone and we’re going to come up with a way to fix your fucking mess,” Jen spoke with so much calmness it terrified her.

“Fuck off, Kale,” she managed, it was more like a croak. Finally blinking her eyes open, Agatha could see the face of her manager and best friend. She was pissed.

“Oh no, Agatha. You fucked up, so, go on.” Jen clapped, the sound irritating Agatha. “Shower, I’ll see you downstairs to fix this in thirty minutes.”

Agatha mumbled her complaint, not daring to say anything to Jen’s face. Her head was dizzy, her stomach felt funny, and she knew when she got up the room would spin. Fuck, she had really drank too much the night before.

“I swear to God, Agatha. I’ll come back up in thirty minutes and dump cold water all over your face and expensive mattress if you don’t do what I say,” her manager yelled from the top of the stairs.

Knowing full well that was a promise more than an empty threat, Agatha got up. Yep, the room was spinning. Shit. She closed her eyes and headed to the bathroom. It was not the first time she had done so while being hungover. She would spend ten minutes under the hot stream of water and then she would begin to shower. It was a practiced dance, at this point.

What worried her was what she had done the night before. Jen was right, she had fucked up big time. Shit. Well, honestly, Agatha was sick of it all. Was it really that big of a deal? Or were people just fishing to get something out of her? How the fuck did people even find out what she did? They would have to be looking. They were always looking at her. What she did, what she didn’t do. How did she do it, if she did it to avoid drama, and just how real can she be when she speaks? Every single detail of her life and her choices were analysed under a fucking microscope, and she would always end up being the bad, egocentric, capitalistic, greedy, out of touch with reality, superstar.

You are not other people, Agatha. A mantra she knew well by now. She let the steam of the shower into her lungs. She had to own up to her actions.

But - really? A couple fucking likes was a big issue to fix accordign to her manager? Could people get their heads out of their asses for just one second? She was sure there were greater things to worry about than Agatha Harkness liking a fucking video on social media about -

Shit.

Yeah, well. Maybe cat’s out of the bag?

Agatha groaned. 

Last night, she had fun. She was happy, she was resting. It was one of those rare moments in which everyone agreed she could have the day off. She didn’t need to attend any after parties, she didn’t need to do anything. So, she got herself comfortable in her house with her alcohol and maybe a joint to keep things peaceful.

She had released her eleventh album back in November, which made it ineligible for this round of Grammys. Therefore, she didn’t need to attend last night’s festivities. They had invited her, fucking obviously. They always did. Not only to the main event, but also to every single after party known to man. Even Beyoncé’s, which was so high-end Agatha had once only dreamt of attending. But, Jen had declined the invitation to every single one of them, citing that Agatha wanted to spend the evening with ‘friends and family’ and see the awards at home. People did not insist, she was known for only being seen when she wanted to be seen. Also, she was supposed to kickstart the last leg of her two year tour around the world next month. It was already being talked about like the biggest tour in history, people understood she was tired and needed rest.

It also gave Jen the night off from manager duties, which allowed her to attend the Grammys with her girlfriend, Alice Wu-Gulliver. Her band was nominated for Best Alternative Music Album, so she was more than happy to leave Agatha alone to go celebrate. That had been Agatha’s winning hand, when they had discussed letting Agatha have the day off.

Given that Wanda, one of Agatha’s oldest friends and recent collaborator in her album, was the favorite to win Record of the Year, Jen had also trusted Agatha with her media phone. It was the phone with all of her official social media accounts. If Wanda won, which she did, by the way, people would expect something from Agatha. One like was not enough. She would need to post something. Most likely a story on Instagram, with some text about how proud and happy she was. Which, Agatha truly was. But she also sent a text to her friend. Privately. One that would never see the light of day. One that was real. Someone else on the team was tasked with sending the approval for the flower arrangement Agatha had chosen a few days before, which should be at Wanda’s by now.

So, against her better judgement, Jen had trusted Agatha with the damned phone. Agatha hated it, too. It was used by Jen with some input from Agatha, and that was all. She did not care to see the endless messages, images, and discourse around her. Even if she had nice fans, lovely people that respected her and defended her online, there were plenty of weird people that sent her threats and hate and just – so much weird stuff. So, Agatha didn’t look at it. Didn’t care for it. Didn’t acknowledge it.

Agatha had her personal phone, with her personal number and her private social media accounts. She only had a few people on them, friends that could be trusted to not sell whatever she posted on them to the tabloids. Her photos were mostly of Scratchy, her rabbit, but still. She only used those accounts to thirst over actresses and musicians she liked.

Which, led to the fucking problem.

During the award ceremony the most gorgeous, perfect, hot woman appeared to present Best Música Urbana Album. She was tanned, had fucking abs which were very much visible thanks to her outfit. She was wearing pinstripe black pants, with a modified vest that only covered her breasts. She was even wearing the matching suit jacket, thrown casually over her shoulders. She had tattoos on the side of her abdomen that trailed down towards her pants. Agatha could barely see they were some type of vine. She was also heavily tatted on the arms and hands, that much was obvious. And then, her face. She had long, brown hair, slicked back on a low pony tail. She had big, caramel eyes with a glint of trouble. She had a cute button nose, and as she stood in front of the microphone, she pushed her tongue against her cheek and smiled, teasingly. Fuck. Agatha was a goner. She had a slight gap in her front teeth that made her fucking feral.

“Please welcome, last year's winner, Rio Vidal,” the voice thundered. A round of applause was heard from the room.

Rio Vidal.

She knew that name. Or, she had heard it, at least. Rio was charming, her voice soft and tender. Her fucking smile - Agatha was soon standing by the big TV screen, staring. She read the nominees, then the winner.

“And the Grammy goes to… Debí Tirar Más Fotos, Benito!” Rio grinned, the camera panned to Benito who smiled and got up to hug his people.

As the narrator commented some stuff about how many Grammys Benito had and how historical his album had been, the angle of the camera opened and she could see Rio standing by the microphone, holding the award in her hands. As Benito got up the stage, Rio handed the award to him, giving him a hug and a quick kiss to the cheek.

“Eh, muchas gracias a toda la gente. Gracias, Rio. Orgullo boricua,” Benito started. Thank you all. Thank you, Rio. Boricua pride.

Huh? Rio was also Puertorican? Shit, Agatha needed to google her.

She grabbed her phone and quickly went over her Wikipedia page. Twenty-eight years old. One Grammy nomination, for her debut album no less, and one win. She apparently also used to play soccer? That was why she was so jacked. Agatha clicked on the Instagram link to Rio’s page.

Holy fucking shit. She was hot. Post after post, Rio posed so effortlessly in every single photo. Agatha zoomed in her arms, tattoos of various plants and flowers adorned her beautiful skin. Shit, there was even a picture of her on a yacht. Rio wore green swim trunks and a black bikini top. Her abdomen on full display in the first picture. She had two sets of vines on each side, almost like a laurel crown. Even in this outfit, Agatha could not see where they ended. She quickly liked the post, scrolling through each of the pictures, zooming in on her face, her arms, her dumb goofy smile in the second photo.

She had to be a lesbian, right? Right? Sure, straight women were dressing like lesbians now more than ever, but Rio had to be gay. Agatha found an announcement for her latest song, she quickly followed Rio on Instagram and then jumped to her music app to listen to her.

It was good. Even if Agatha did not speak Spanish, she googled the lyrics to what Rio said. The Awards were now a background noise. Alice had already won, and Wanda’s was the last of the evening. She could go down the rabbit hole of Rio Vidal and then get back to paying attention. Agatha finished her excuse for a paloma, letting the beat and the smooth voice of Rio fill her brain.

Baby, what’s up?

Got a girlfriend or got a boyfriend?

If you get tired of him, mami, I’ll be his replacement.

Oh, definitely a lesbian. Thank God.

You’re fire and I look like a badass in the mirror.

In the mirror and everywhere, apparently.

She got up, moving her hips to the beat, and kept on reading the translation of Rio’s words. As she prepared her next paloma at the bar nook, Rio and the backup singer’s voice almost made her drop the tequila.

Tell me, mami, what do you like? Fast it grows.

I like that Rio baby kisses me down there.

Oh.

Suddenly, the image of Rio’s face between Agatha’s legs made her wobbly. The song continued, Agatha finished her drink and drank it quickly. She made the next one. Shit. She needed to learn Spanish. She was sure it sounded better in Rio’s mother tongue. Mmm, Rio’s tongue.

The song ended and Agatha headed back to the couch. She fished out her phone again and went to TikTok. She typed Rio Vidal and hit the first suggestion it gave her: Rio Vidal dancing.

Oh holy mother of lesbians.

Rio could dance. From what Agatha knew, she could dance really well. Her hips moved to the beat of the song, she rolled her body just right, Rio put her hands in front of her, holding her mic, smirking to the crowd that filmed her, and her hips snapped. Agatha could hear the screams of women in every video, she couldn’t blame them.

Then, she got one that made her feel insane. Rio had backup dancers in her concerts. Beautiful, sexy women in tiny clothes all got in a line, Rio in the middle of them. All of their hips pressed together, moving to the beat. The video said ‘Un perreito de Rio Vidal y se te reinicia la vida.’ She translated it, a little dance from Rio Vidal and your life restarts.

Huh, Agatha felt like ‘little dance’ was not right. But, she got the idea. Another video, Rio now sitting down on a couch on stage, a woman in fucking lingerie straddled her. Rio kept on singing as she held the dancer by the waist and she dipped back. Agatha felt jealous of all of these women, these professional dancers that were obviously having fun on stage and working, that got to dance like that to Rio. That Rio reciprocated to, and moved her hips against their asses.

Agatha liked a bunch of videos, staring at all of them. From what she gathered, Rio enjoyed performing in loose cargo pants with a tight, matching top. It allowed movement, it punctuated her hip movements, and her tattoos and insane body was on full display.

She dropped her phone and decided that being aware of the existence of Rio Vidal meant trouble for her. She kept on drinking, paying attention to the ceremony on her TV.

Agatha Harkness had risen to fame at sixteen years old. Her first album, a country album at that, had sold over forty thousand copies on its release week. From there, every single album had dominated every top hit list. The Wikipedia section for her Awards and Accomplishments was bigger than some states’ full pages. She had beaten every single record possible, which led to now having to make the distinction of ‘the first male artist to…’

She loved it. She loved music. She loved creating and making songs and writing about her feelings and thoughts and ideas. It hadn’t been easy. On the contrary. Agatha had worked her ass off every single day. First, she had begged her mom to help her sign a record deal. Evanora had agreed to drive her to a meeting she had secured through e-mails with a record label, where her mom had painted the picture of a perfect single mom who took care of her daughter.

Once Evanora realized the mine gold she was sitting on, she made it a point to help Agatha rise to fame. At the beginning, her mom had had most of the control over her, as she was still a child. Once Agatha turned eighteen, and her third album had secured her first Album of the Year Grammy, they had fought about her having independence, and had come to some agreement. Evanora was now the Director of her Management Company, while Agatha, as the talent and main owner, held some say in her actions.

Now that they were filthy rich, Evanora only played the part of doting mother when cameras were around. Agatha despised her, but knew that playing the part of a loving family was the easier route for the media. However, her mother’s presence also meant one thing. She could not come out publicly.

Evanora knew Agatha was a lesbian. They had fought plenty of times for Agatha to change the gender of a song. She had to change pronouns to ‘you’ when ‘her’ was the obvious rhyme. Agatha had to work around either not gendering anything or ending the song with something to allude that it was all from a man’s point of view.

As she got older, and richer, Evanora did not care as much as she once did. Agatha got to sing at fund raisers for LGBTQ+ rights, she got to sing in a leotard pink and orange, the colors of the lesbian flag. She made statements about voting for equal rights, she supported up and coming queer artists, just like Alice and her band, for example.

Still - the fear was ingrained in her. A part of Agatha believed that if she were to come out, all of this, all of her success and money and respect and legacy, would crumble. That people would stop listening to her music, claiming that they could no longer relate to her experiences. That fans would call her out on not being honest before. Or, even worse, that people would accuse her of faking being a lesbian just to gain more following. That she decided to step on small queer artists by coming out and stealing their thunder.

That was what always happened with her. Anything she did, people would say she did it for her own benefit. That she wasn’t real, that she actually didn’t care, that the only thing she cared about was her money and breaking records and being privileged.

Agatha Harkness was a myth. Agatha Harkness was an idea. Too big to be a real person. Too much of an enigma to have real feelings, real thoughts. Too much money and too much fame.

Once Wanda was on stage with her Grammy in her hand, Agatha turned off her TV and started to look for her media phone. She would repost the official post from the Grammy’s Instagram page and come up with some text to go with it. She headed to the kitchen, the last place she remembered seeing it.

Agatha’s house was big and private. She bought it with the money she made from the songs she wrote about her life, thank you very much. The TV room she was in was at the back of the house, so she headed to the front towards the open space kitchen. It was what she liked to call a ‘Nancy Meyers’ kitchen’. It was lived in. Agatha loved to cook and she made sure to have everything and anything she might need.

As she left her used cup on the sink, she turned towards the white marble table in the middle. She moved a few papers around, she had been brainstorming new ideas for her next album during dinner. Agatha suddenly saw the phone on top of the table.

Fucking shit.

The phone there was her own phone. Her personal phone. The one with her private accounts. Which meant that the phone she had been using, that was now somewhere on the couch in the TV room was -

Shit.

Agatha sprinted to the room she had come out of. Both phones were always on quiet mode when Agatha had a day off. If Jen or anyone wanted something from her, they’d need to come to her house and ask her directly. She took the phone and dared to lift off the notification center from the blocked page.

TikTok +100 notifications.

X +100 notifications.

Google Alert: Agatha Harkness, preview.

Fuck.

Agatha had quickly posted her congratulations to Wanda and turned off the damn phone. She had gone to sleep after sending Wanda a text from her personal phone, which she also turned off soon after.

And now, as she quickly got dressed in some sweats and an oversized shirt, letting her hair air dry, she bit her lip trying to come up with something to get out of this.

She hadn’t seen anything, but she could picture it. First, people that would have gotten the notification of @agathaharkness liked your video on TikTok. Then, people would surely go over to Rio’s profile and see that not only had she followed her yesterday, but also that she had gone on a liking spree on almost a year’s worth of posts. Shit.

Agatha went downstairs, the door to her office was open. She walked in, Jen was on the phone with someone as she drank from a bottle of water. Jen pointed to the desk, a matching one was waiting for Agatha. She drank, the salty flavor of the electrolyte hitting her dry mouth with a sense of relief.

She let Jen finish her conversation and walked to the piano on the corner of the room. This was the smaller one, not used to compose new music, but rather used to ease her mind when work got tiring. There was a separate room with all of her equipment and sound proof walls for the Agatha Harkness. This was just for Agatha.

She played a few notes, mind wandering. Would it really be a bad thing? So what if people knew she liked Rio? She liked plenty of artists. Fuck, even Benito was her friend! They talked! Maybe they could say she was - uhm - appreciating other genres?

No, that sounded bad.

“Agatha, what the fuck were you thinking?” Jen got her out of her reverie.

Her head hit the keys. “I got high and thought I was using my phone, not that phone,” Agatha whispered. “Look, Jen, I’m sorry. I haven’t looked yet, but I’m sure there’s an angle here we can work?”

“Well, I remember that, yes, but -”

“No, Agatha. Your whole brand for like, almost ten years now, is that you don’t follow no one on Instagram. Not your friends, not the label, not people you collaborate with, not even your rumored boyfriends. Shit, Agatha, you don’t even follow Harkness Station.”

Agatha groaned. Her hands now in her hair, pressing herself against the keys. Jen was right. After an altercation with a rapper that turned into a hate campaign against her, she had disappeared from social media. She’d come back with a banger of an album that awarded her second Album of the Year Grammy, but she had maintained her distance ever since. She didn’t follow anyone back. Her 165 million followers sat nicely next to her 0 following. She liked it, it was her thing. She didn’t even follow the account her team used for marketing, Harkness Station. She didn’t even follow Wanda.

Now, due to her drunken and high stupor, she followed one person in the whole world. Rio Vidal.

“And it’s not like you can just unfollow her now, people will talk even more.”

“I get it, Jen. What can we do?” She lifted her head from the piano. Agatha could feel the marks on her forehead from where she had pressed too hard.

“I was on the phone with Management. They think you should follow some of the people you’re friendly with, maybe get up to ten people. Then, we’ll make a dinner party for them and post about it,” Jen spoke slowly, making sure Agatha understood her. “It’ll go with the theme of your last album, we’ll follow them at different, key moments, and follow the same pattern of liking stuff about them on TikTok.”

“Okay so, we’ll pretend it was all a marketing scheme,” Agatha sighed.

“Yes, once the dinner party ends, we’ll unfollow them and act like nothing happened.”

Agatha got up and sat at her chair on the desk. Jen followed her, sitting in front of her. She took her tablet from the desk and went on.

“Here’s the list of possibilities: Wanda Maximoff, Alice Wu-Gulliver, Bad Bunny, Lilia Calderu, Carol Danvers, Natasha Romanoff, Steve Rogers, Peter Parker, Matt Murdock, and, well, Rio Vidal.” Jen finished.

Agatha didn’t even like most of them. Well, the men at least. She only tolerated Benito and Peter, they were nice, easy to talk to. Matt was mostly a producer she hadn’t had the opportunity to work with, but could be interested. He had his own band, but had gained popularity by being the producer with more Grammy nominations in history. Steve, on the other hand, was a country singer who made ways by covering an old lesbian song and calling it ‘a tribute’. She hated how now people only knew it because of him, and not for the original singer.

Wanda, Alice, Carol, Lilia - all her friends. She was friendly with Nat at awards shows, but they had never had the opportunity to really talk. People used to say Nat was the up and coming Agatha Harkness, but it hadn’t truly happened yet. Not when Agatha was still making music. Still, they praised each other in interviews and whatnot. Even if Agatha was not really close to Nat, she liked her music well enough.

Every single person on that list made sense. They were all either in pop, alternative/indie or música urbana. Only one person stood out like a sore thumb.

“Why Steve?”

Jen sighed and dropped her eyes from Agatha’s face.

“Jen? Why is Steve on the list?” She pressed.

“Evanora insisted he might be a good distraction. You know, for a rumor.”

“No, Jen.” Agatha got up, maybe a bit too fast. She held onto the desk. She was still hungover, after all. She sighed. “I don’t want that.”

“I tried to argue, but I got overpowered.” Her friend lifted her shoulders in surrender. There was really nothing she could do against Evanora and the board. Even Agatha was aware of that.

“We had a deal. After the whole Peter Quill fiasco, we had a deal.” Agatha pointed a finger to her friend.

The famous Quill-gate, as she liked to call it. She had been 27, reborn had won Album of the Year at the Grammys, making Agatha a two-time winner of the biggest award of the night. She felt on top of the world, as she could go out again into the world, confidence regained.

It hadn’t hurt that during that time she had had a relationship with Hela, a world-known DJ. Agatha had loved her so much. She was fun and adventurous and older and hot. It worked well for them. DJs, even the most famous ones, were not as photographed and followed around as popstars. Hela understood Agatha’s need to remain private and always respected her decision to remain in the closet for the public, as she wasn’t as public with her private life as well.

Agatha had started to write her seventh album, Amore, all inspired by her relationship. But, as soon as she presented the idea to Management, Evanora jumped. Her mother claimed that she could not release an album about love and a relationship if the public did not know about her relationship. Her suggestion of coming out with Hela had been buried, and out of nowhere Peter Quill had appeared.

He was also a DJ slash producer. More in love with the public eye, always appearing on red carpets and events, arm around whichever popstar had granted him a hit. He wanted to make it big as a solo artist, but so far only his collaborations went viral. He hated it, all of the industry knew.

After a discussion that turned into a breakup with Hela, Agatha was soon all dolled up and sitting in front of this man grabbing dinner. The paparazzi outside the restaurant had been all called up for, obviously. The worst part of it all, was that Agatha truly had to pretend to be in the relationship for real. Peter didn’t know, no one had briefed him that it was all a gimmick. They had told him she was interested in meeting him and that was all he knew.

Agatha released her album, had to swallow her pride and talk about how in love she was with Quill, how much they complimented each other with music, and how much of an inspiration he had been. All lies.

Their relationship lasted two years. Enough to roll out the album, to go to that year’s Grammys, where Agatha won Song of the Year for her hit Like No One’s Watching. A song she wrote about going out partying with Hela in Berlin, where no one would recognize them and they could dance and make out in clubs freely.

She had to pretend it was about accompanying Quill to his raves in Las Vegas, and how many eyes were always on them, but their love made it feel like they were alone. Even straight shit felt boring.

In the end, he broke up with her because her indie album won her for the third time Album of the Year, and he still couldn’t produce a hit on his own. Agatha didn’t care enough to try and fix the relationship, and was too happy that it had been his decision. That way, not even Evanora could force her to continue faking it even in her private life.

After a surge in her fame thanks to the following two albums, Evanora had agreed that she would not push Agatha to maintain another fake relationship. Agatha had agreed to not write that much about love, and focus mainly on writing about wanting to be loved. Which, ultimately, was the truth. It had worked, Evanora did not try to make Agatha date anyone else.

Until now.

“Jen, she cannot be for real. He’s -” Ugly? A man? Blond? “He’s not my type at all. Not even my pretend type,” her voice trembled.

She was fucking thirty five and her mother still was dictating her life? Not even the millions of dollars in her bank account made up for it.

“I know, Agatha. That’s why you won’t date him. During the dinner you’ll just have to pretend to be a bit interested in him, we’ll take pictures and he will post some of them. Fans will run off with whatever breadcrumb they think they’ve found and that’d be all.” Jen’s fingers move on the tablet at lightning speed. “It’s all scheduled. We’ll start with Alice and Wanda today, given that they’re your friends. Then on Tuesday it’s Lilia and Carol, also your friends. Matt and Peter, Wednesday and Thursday, Nat on Friday, Steve on Saturday, and we’ll end with Bad Bunny on Sunday.”

Agatha furrowed her brows. “Why do we have to hurry with this?”

“Oh, Sunday’s the Superbowl. Bad Bunny will be the half time show, so it seems appropriate.” Right. Agatha knew that was happening, it was all over the news.

She nodded. “I guess it’ll be easier, I can also post something in support of him and I’m sure his performance will be great, so we can also praise that, too.”

“Yes, Agatha. We’ll get this fixed. Don’t you worry about it.” Jen stood up to her and held her arms.

“I can’t believe I fucked up this big.” Agatha was not a hugger, nor was Jen. So maybe it was the alcohol in both of their systems, or maybe it was the fact that for the first time in hours they seemed to have calmed down, when she let herself lean against Jen in a hug.

“I know, Agatha. And on Alice’s big night too,” she whispered.

“Yeah, I congratulated her.”

“I saw. It was cute. Thank you.” Jen gave her two quick light slaps on the back, straightening her up. “Now, let’s get to work, superstar.”

Agatha groaned. She hated that word. She sat down again as she listened to Jen go on about optics and how they’d start soon with liking Alice’s posts and following her. Agatha’s mind was only half present, as the one thought that lingered was I’ll get to have dinner with Rio Vidal.

This time around, Agatha had decided to get strong for the tour. AGATHA HARKNESS: FOLLOWING SPREE ENDS WITH HOTTEST MAN ALIVE, COUNTRY SINGER STEVE ROGERS. IS LOVE IN THE AIR FOR THE SUPERSTAR?

WILL THE COVENLESS WITCH FINALLY HAVE A PLUS ONE? HARKNESS FOLLOWS ROGERS ON SOCIAL MEDIA, SPARKING RUMORS.

It wasn’t common for Agatha to ruminate through social media commentary. At least that’s what she had learned over twenty years with this career. They had an agreement, though. Jen would always share some with her, just for her to be aware of the overall discussion that was happening online. Not necessarily for Agatha to respond to it, but rather so she could prepare and come up with ways to change the narrative if needed.

The outcome of Agatha following Steve Rogers was expected. It always happened whenever a male artist was considered to be in close proximity with her. Even when she had first worked with Billy, the gayest man alive, people had wondered whether there was something going on between them. A part of Agatha was also fairly sure this was the scenario Evanora had pictured when she’d suggested Steve Rogers for this whole ruse. She could even picture her mother feeding some lies to the tabloids herself. 

After a few papers had been thrown in her office and Jen had done her best to limit Agatha to damaging only things without importance (a Grammy had to be taken out of Agatha’s hands at some point), she had decided to calm the fuck down. Just because the press and the internet had decided that she had the hots for a dumb redneck man didn’t mean she actually had the hots for him. It was just a necessary evil she had to participate in so she could get her prize: Rio.

Agatha could do it. She had done it before. If she had been in a PR relationship with someone as dumb as Peter Quill, she could absolutely pose with Steve Rogers and gift him the highest amount of press he would ever get in his life, even if it was just a crinkle in hers. She could pretend in front of him so he could then tell a funny story of hers in an interview. She could separate her true self from the person she was supposed to be, if it meant that eventually she could be happy.

Could she? Could Agatha be happy? She had the money, the praise, the fame. Her name was already carved in the history of music. There was no one else like her, and it would take decades for someone to overtake her. She would always be the too-high record that no one would ever break. But she was lonely.

It was lonely to have to pretend. To have to smile and shrug whenever she was asked about her dating life. It was lonely to be one of the world’s most famous people alive and yet to have absolutely no one actually know her. Sure she had friends, but Agatha craved another type of intimacy. To have someone know her so deeply, to notice the things about her she didn’t even know. She wanted someone who would hold her left hand when she started to crack her knuckles with her thumb whenever she got anxious. She wanted someone to notice how she would murmur a tune for days before actually writing down an idea for a new song. Someone who would preemptively fill her water bottle at night before bed. Someone who would open their arms and let her fall into them without a word, and provide the needed pressure she sometimes needed to calm her racing mind.

She wanted someone who did all of that out of love, out of want. Someone for whom knowing her came easily, naturally. Someone who willingly wanted to get to know Agatha, to see her. Someone who cared for her as a person, without expecting her to perform.

And she knew. Agatha was well aware. She could never have that with a woman and ask her to keep it private. Not only that, but to ask her to not take it personally if Agatha had to deny their relationship to the public. No one would ever agree, and truthfully, Agatha would never ask anyone for that.

So, here she was. Sick of her own predicament. Kept in a golden cage of her own doing. Forcing herself to go to the length of coming up with a dumb dinner just so the world wouldn’t look too much into her drunken mistake. She already knew she would pose with Steve Rogers, she would charm him and get him to post the picture on his social media. She would remain quiet as the world came up with whatever stories they wanted to invent. She wouldn’t deny nor confirm anything. She would be a passive participant in her own life, just as she had been doing for her whole career.

Agatha knew the media would go crazy. They would try and come up with the most forced invisible string theories she would ever read or hear. And she would let them. Agatha wouldn’t be lying to them, at least not directly. Her silence would be interpreted however people pleased. In a few years, whenever people realized that she hadn’t actually interacted further with Steve, it would all go away and become part of some dark and forgotten conspiracy theory about herself.

That’s what she told herself, either way.

The truth - the thoughts that gnawed Agatha’s brain every now and then - was that she was scared. How many lies could she accept people believing? She had never actually said she was straight, no one had ever asked her directly. Interviewers always wanted to know about the men she ‘dated’, they tried to come up with weird and elaborate questions about her muses, they would try and trap her into admitting having a relationship with any and every single man she had ever gone out to dinner with.

And, sure, it was all part of the grand plan of having her legacy perfectly clean, without any drama caused by herself. Because that was who Agatha Harkness was. Agatha Harkness was a hard worker who loved writing songs and perfecting her craft. Any drama she found herself tangled in wasn’t of her doing, it was always someone else who wanted to drag her into: the rapper from when she was younger, Peter Quill, even the so-called animosity between herself and Natasha Romanoff was a product of online discourse. Neither she nor Natasha had ever talked about it, or had done anything directly to feed the narrative.

But, Agatha still laid awake at night wondering just when could she truly live? Would there ever come a time where she could date someone and not be afraid of it being public? Would she ever find someone she could trust, could actually date? She’d had flings with different women, from other high profile closeted women to musicians that would puff and huff when she asked them to sign an NDA. It wasn’t easy, but it had to be done. Either way, every single woman was too eager to get fucked by Agatha Harkness that every fling was merely physical. No one had ever tried to have something… real with her. Not since Hela, who oftentimes also felt like she only kept Agatha around as a younger hot thing to flash around every now and then.

Evanora had made that clear. Agatha was not a product of love, so perhaps she was destined from her conception to never be able to receive it. She could only look in people’s windows and see their warm and cozy lives, filled with eachotherness, while she stayed outside all alone.

Maybe Agatha Harkness was just someone who gave others the words to express themselves, and she was never to receive them in return.

Maybe she was destined to just be present in others’ milestones, staying forever the same in a glass window.

Maybe she was only the soundtrack of a beautiful life, without ever having lived one.

There was a famous picture of her, back when she still did meet and greets before her concerts, where she was just standing there in the background as a guy proposed to his girlfriend. Yeah, Agatha felt like that was an accurate representation of her life.

“Babe, are you okay?”

Could a week be more exhausting? Agatha had worked her ass off on her down season to improve her body stamina, and she was not able to get out of bed on Monday morning after her first week back. She decided, because she was strong enough to decide for herself, that it was mere physical exhaustion and not the fact that the only thing she'd heard from Rio was a bunch of pictures of her and Nat having a great time back in LA.

That first concert in Gelsenkirchen had been torture; for the first time in her career she'd wondered about the fine print of her contract where the repercussions of cancelling the following dates were stipulated. In the middle of Acts Five and Six she had smiled widely and waved excitedly at the VIP tent, pretending to be oh so happy at the sight of a ginormous blonde man that stood out like a sore thumb among her delicately chosen guests. At the end of the concert, Agatha had gotten off stage, all sweaty and tired, and had hugged Steve like her life depended on it. Together they'd walked backstage, an awkward hello had been had, and they'd gotten inside different vehicles to get driven to their different hotels.

Rio hadn't answered her text, presumably she'd seen the whole Steve at The Witchy Tour thing and hadn't liked it. The next day, when her Twitter feed and TikTok For You Page were filled with snippets and pictures of her clinging on Steve's arms that made her want to puke her heart out, Agatha had also seen that back home Rio had gone out with Nat.

Thankfully, Scratchy was in her lap so she didn't get up yelling. Agatha could have decorum in front of her child, even if her heart was truly breaking. The other guests in the hotel wouldn't appreciate if Agatha Harkness screamed from the top of her lungs, either.

The other two nights at Gelsenkirchen had been the same. Pretend to be enchanted by Steve's presence, get off stage and run to him, wave at her fans and go to her hotel room to soak in the tub for two hours and wonder just how big of a deal it would be if she drowned there.

Each night she would get out of the tub and put on a pair of boxers with a black t-shirt with pink, bubbly letters that looked like graffiti that read XOXO Tour. A parting gift Rio hadn't even realized she'd given her, left crumbled on the bathroom floor. The same shirt Rio had wore the last night they'd spent together and that Agatha was starting to fear needed to be washed.

Agatha hadn't given Rio her private accounts just because she used those accounts to stalk her. She followed plenty of Young Rio Fan Account that she didn't want the woman to know about. Agatha would fall asleep with a reel on loop on what she knew now where edits of the woman. Other times, she'd find interviews online just to see Rio laugh on camera, and Agatha was so strong by enduring seeing her Rio flirt with whomever was interviewing her.

That's how Agatha stumbled upon the only video on TikTok she'd saved on her own phone. It was a Genius Lyrics interview, Rio was sitting on stool, wearing jorts and a muscle top with Shakira's face on it, it read Como Montañas on it. Rio was singing one of her latest songs, which meant that the interview was either before they'd met or just after, was this Rio unaware of how Agatha tasted, or was the permanent smirk on her face a product of being familiar with the moans of the biggest popstar in the world? She hadn't decided yet which option was more devastating. Rio looked especially delicious, flawless make-up, her hair looked soft and she was man-spreading in a way that the jeans jutted in-between her legs. Agatha wanted to pounce on her.

Yes. She was. She missed Rio, even if she was so fucking angry at her for taking Nat out on dates.

Why the fuck was she taking Nat out on dates, anyway? One dinner to confuse the press, she could understand that. But they'd been going out all weekend. Picture after picture, video after video. Agatha had seen it, and since she'd interacted with it, her whole feed was now post after post of both of them together. They'd gone out for dinner at first, Rio's profile was photographed through the glass of the place they'd gone to. Paps had even followed them on their little stroll until they entered a bar. What Agatha disliked the most, she feared, was that she was getting to see Rio's date outfits online and not in real life.

The next day, more pap photos of them together around town. Nat was photographed dragging Rio by the hand into a coffee shop, and Rio had two books in her hand, laughing mid-photo. The Young Rio fan accounts had edited the picture to add an overlaying text that read "If you see me being dragged by a mean femme, leave me there, it's exactly where I want to be."

That day she almost lost her phone by throwing it to the wall. Thankfully, she was tired and it didn't get too far. The TikTok some girl had posted about meeting them in the coffee shop and how lovely they were! made her nauseous. Fucking moron.

The last thing she'd seen of the pair was a zoomed in video of two figures moving around a soccer field. Nat, the blonde person on the video, was laughing, while Rio, the black haired individual, was running around with a ball at her feet. Agatha's blood boiled over to the point of it dripping out of her. Metaphorically, of course. Seriously? Did Rio only had one idea of a funny date? Apparently, and not because she'd dared to look at the videos, Nat and Rio had also gone out to lunch and to a nightclub on the weekend.

Agatha Harkness was fucking furious, especially because she couldn't quite complain. She had actually been so mature and demure and hadn't texted Rio any of the messages she'd written where she called her all types of names. Two knocks on her bedroom door signaled her that in ten minutes her masseuse would arrive to work on the knots on her back. The first step of her recovery process after a day of traveling and laying around. Then came some more physical therapy, voice therapy, and then she could start thinking about dancing around Munich semi-professionally

The purple bubbles that represented her own messages reminded her of a giddy Rio taking the time to personalize her application as much as possible, her own way of hoping Agatha would actually use it instead of only communicating through iMessage.

She was squatted in position, ready for the elevator to push her up towards the stage. She swallowed and exhaled. March nineteen. The changes had been done, the medleys had been made. There was no going back.

"Five, four, three…" The elevator shot her up and the audience went wild. Agatha Harkness smiled at them, letting them appreciate the first part of her signaling.

"Yes, the change is ready, go!" One of the assistants replied and quickly sent her on her way back to the stage.

The Fifth Act was for reborn. The lights on the stadium were programmed to be white and bright blue during the four songs of the act, alternating to fit the mood. The first song was white, which Agatha hadn't asked to change. But, as soon as the lights shimmered down and Agatha stood in complete darkness, she smiled tenderly, prepared for the spotlight to illuminate her face.

As it did so, the music started, and the stadium filled with green and purple lights coming from the audience's bracelets.

"This ain't for the best, my reputation's never been worse, so you must like me for me." Agatha sang with as much emotion as she could muster.

Maybe she'd written it for Hela a long time ago, but now it felt accurate to her situation with Rio.

The crowd went wild, probably some of them had noticed the changed in colors, especially her die-hard fans who knew every detail of the tour.

"We can't make, any promises, now, can we, babe? But you can make me a drink," she let the space for the crowd to shout back at her the call-back. Agatha laughed, her nervousness dissipating entirely. The stadium looked beautiful.

She carried out with the performance, knowing that the next moment would make the message clearer for Rio.

The stadium went black again and she walked to the center of her catwalk. She turned to the audience and the lights turned completely green. The pretty shade that was Rio's favorite.

"Don't blame me, love made me crazy, if it doesn't you ain't doing it right." Agatha lifted her arm as part of the choreography. "Lord, save me, my drug is my baby, I'll be usin' for the rest of my life."

The whole light sequence was the same, just completely green. Agatha smiled as wide as possible, it was so pretty. Was Rio looking at her? Probably following the concert through a live stream? She hoped she was - Agatha's tour was the biggest thing in her career. She'd designed it in a way that every single attendee would live the same experience, no matter where they'd seen her. Someone in Singapore would get the exact same show as someone in Brazil, and she'd perform with the same amount of energy, dedication, and love.

However, the people in Munich on March 19 would never know that they were receiving more intention than anyone else in the whole tour.

Agatha had spent two days trying out different combinations of songs she'd thought about, until she found what she felt was the best. "I know you're one of the hardest working person in the industry, and you made it look so easy people complain about you. You are always writing down ideas and singing in your phone, do you think I don't notice when you wake up at two just so you don't forget it?" Rio searched her eyes and continued. "Agatha, you are bigger and brighter than all the people who talk badly about you, you are literally making history as we speak. And I know this is not even close to the end."

"You are too sweet," Agatha said. She moved to removed her legs from Rio's lap, but the Puertorican held her in place. "I need to go pee, darling."

"Not before you tell me you agree with me."

"What?"

"Mi corazón, tell me I'm right."

Agatha moved her legs with more intention, not caring if she hit Rio's thighs. "I don't want to, I'll pee here if you don't let me go."

Two hands lifted from her legs and Agatha sat straight, getting up saying, "See? Was that so hard?"

Rio shook her head slowly. "Si quieres tener el control, puedes tener el control, mami. Yo solo quiero hacerte feliz, lo sabes, ¿no?" (Translation: If you want to have control, you can have it, mami. I just want to make you happy, you know that, don't you?)

"Yes, yes, I am amazing and the best laid ever and I'm making history and yada-yada. I'll pee and then we shower?" The responding sound of Rio hastily getting up made her laugh. "Oh, did I say puppy's favorite words?"

"Woof, woof."

Laughter followed them to the bathroom, Agatha felt lighter and happier as she enjoyed these last hours with a radiant woman who liked her well enough.

Agatha was particularly sweatier tonight than any other night. The humidity was crazy, her hair was big and frizzy and all over her place, only some strands stuck to her face with sweat. She smiled at the crowd as she put on the guitar on her frame.

This mash-up had not been heard or approved by anyone. Agatha had just thought of the songs in the shower, the melodies intertwining as Rio gave her another orgasm under the water.

"Hello, Warsaw!" A quick glance at the VIP tent let her know that Rio was clapping and shouting with the rest of the crowd.

This night, her other guests were movie stars Rio had gone pale over when Jen had read the final list an hour before the show started. What do you mean Anne Hathaway is going to be here? Anne Hathaway? Princess Mia? What the fuck!

Aside from the obvious hint of jealousy Agatha felt, she'd mainly laughed at her Grammy-winner… Rio… losing her shit over a famous actress. When would she realize that she also belonged to these circles? Agatha chuckled as she saw all of the VIP guests close to one another, were they hugging? It appeared as if Rio had gotten them to put their arms around each other and move to the beat of the crowd.

Agatha smiled with more intention, remembering the thousands of fans waiting for her. "Are you ready?" The responding screams made her laughed and she said, "I hope you know this one."

She started singing one of her favorite songs on her newest album, the fact that the crowd screamed and sang it right back at her made the decision worth it, and as she looked again to the VIP tent, the toothy smile made it feel like she had done very, very good.

My boredom's bone deep
This cage was once just fine
Am I allowed to cry?
I dream of cracking locks
Throwing my life to the wolves
Or the ocean rocks
Crashing into him tonight
He's a paradox
I'm seeing visions, am I bad?
Or mad? Or wise?
What if he's written 'mine' on my upper thigh
Only in my mind?

Agatha played it a beat too fast, a necessity to make it fit with the song she'd planned to mix it with. She sang a chorus, and then another verse, before shifting to one of her favorite songs.

Untouchable, burning brighter than the sun
And when you're close, I feel like comin' undone.

The crowd roared, Agatha had seen online it was one of the missing pieces of the tour, according to several of the fan pages she followed. Tonight, it wasn't being sung for her fans, though. It was entirely for one single woman in the crowd.

In the middle of the night, wakin' from this dream
I wanna feel you by my side, standing' next to me
You gotta come on, come on, say that we'll be together

They had failed, again, to discuss what their thing was. Agatha didn't want to discuss it, fearing that she'd say the wrong thing again and that Rio would leave looking sad and lost. She also didn't want to talk about it, because, well, she didn't know what to say, exactly.

She strung with more strength and returned to the first song, hoping that this proclamation was heard not only by the people in Warsaw, but by everyone who actually listened to her. Maybe, just maybe, Agatha Harkness was starting to accept something that she needed the rest of the world to also come to understand.

What if I roll the stone away?
They're gonna crucify me anyway
What if the way you hold me
Is actually what's holy?
If long suffering propriety
Is what they want from me
They don't know how you've haunted me
So stunningly
I choose you and me
... Religiously

She kept on playing the guitar but she walked across the catwalk until the point where she was closest to the VIP tent. She smiled at all of them, but kept her eyes locked with the caramel pools that now beamed up at her.

Once that first part of her Acoustic Set finished, she took off the guitar and put it down, engulfed by shouts and screams. Agatha swayed her re-worn green dress and sat down on the stool. She dared another glance at the VIP tent and saw that Rio, even if whom seemed to be her favorite actress in the world was standing next to her talking to her, was not taking her eyes off of her.

That soft, tender smile she'd seen only on particular moments with Rio, like at breakfast that morning. The way her eyes twinkled with something other than fun - something neither had said aloud.

Agatha winked at her and began pressing the keys, smiling up at the blinding lights from the stage and the thousands of phones recording her. She sat back straight, willing to be captured at her fullest, brightest, and happiest.
